unable to play or engage in leisure activities quietly.\n  manifestation: Even when I’m doing something I like, like editing photos or sketching, I can’t keep it quiet for long—I start tapping, And then I’ll realize I missed the part that actually mattered.  \n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly—I’m not trying to cut people off. I’m not sure how much of it counts as a problem versus just me needing a better system.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking, like grabbing something or doing something physical without considering the consequences?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think I’m, like, grabbing things in a dangerous way or anything. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah—thanks. I guess I’m here because I’ve been having a really hard time sitting still, like in the radiology suite or in class. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it kind of feels like I’m crawling out of my skin when I’m supposed to stay put. I’ve tried to just push through it for months, but I still end up pacing, shifting around, or finding some reason to get up and move. I’m not sure if that makes sense, but it’s been getting hard to manage.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice you were having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I think it goes back pretty far, honestly. My parents still bring up my elementary school conferences—teachers would say I was kind of always out of my seat, or calling out, or rushing ahead before they’d even finished the directions. I don’t mean it like I was trying to be a problem, but yeah… it was happening way before middle school, not just since I started radiology training.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble paying attention to details or making careless mistakes?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think that’s the main thing I notice, if that makes sense. Like schoolwork, or at work?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others or blurt out answers before they've finished speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, sometimes. I try not to, because I know it probably comes off rude. Like if a supervisor is explaining a positioning step or asking me something, I’ll jump in with what I think the answer is before they’re done talking. And then I’ll realize I missed the part that actually mattered.  \n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly—I’m not trying to cut people off. It just kind of comes out before I stop myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or managing your time?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try to keep things together. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often fidget with your hands or feet or feel restless when you're sitting or standing still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one is probably more of an issue for me. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but when I’m supposed to sit still in the radiology suite or in class, I feel really restless, like I’m crawling out of my skin.\n\nI’ll shift around a lot, move my feet, or find some excuse to get up or pace if I can. I’ve tried to just tough it out for months, but it still happens. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when it's not your turn or when others are trying to speak?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I can talk a lot if I’m nervous or if I’m really into what we’re talking about, but I try pretty hard not to interrupt people.\n\nI’m not sure if other people would say I do that more than I realize, though. I don’t want to answer it wrong. Like, I haven’t noticed it being a big problem, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have strong emotions that are hard to control?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I can get into something if it’s interesting, like anyone, and then realize some time passed… but I don’t know if it’s to the point of losing track of everything else.\n\nI wouldn’t want to overstate it. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often fidget with your hands or feet or feel restless when you're sitting or standing still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one is probably more of an issue for me. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but when I’m supposed to sit still in the radiology suite or in class, I feel really restless, like I’m crawling out of my skin.\n\nI’ll shift around a lot, move my feet, or find some excuse to get up or pace if I can. I’ve tried to just tough it out for months, but it still happens. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when it's not your turn or when others are trying to speak?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I can talk a lot if I’m nervous or if I’m really into what we’re talking about, but I try pretty hard not to interrupt people.\n\nI’m not sure if other people would say I do that more than I realize, though. I don’t want to answer it wrong. Like, I haven’t noticed it being a big problem, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have strong emotions that are hard to control?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I get frustrated or stressed like anyone, but I don’t know that I’d say my emotions are “out of control.”\n\nSometimes I probably feel things pretty strongly, but I try to keep it to myself and not make it other people’s problem. I guess I’d want to know what you mean by hard to control—like yelling, crying, shutting down, that kind of thing?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on something that you get completely absorbed in it and lose track of everything else?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I can get into something if it’s interesting, like anyone, and then realize some time passed… but I don’t know if it’s to the point of losing track of everything else.\n\nI wouldn’t want to overstate it. It’s not something I’ve really thought of as a major issue, I guess. What kind of situations do you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget things you're supposed to do, like appointments or chores?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try not to. I use reminders on my phone for appointments and stuff, so I usually manage okay.\n\nChores are kind of harder to judge because sometimes I’ll just put them off or forget until someone reminds me, but I don’t know if that’s more than normal. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Honestly, I think it’s more the sitting still part. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but that’s kind of why I came in.

I’ve tried to just push through it for months, but I still end up pacing, shifting around, or finding some reason to get up and move. I’m not sure if that makes sense, but it’s been getting hard to manage.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice you were having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I think it goes back pretty far, honestly. My parents still bring up my elementary school conferences—teachers would say I was kind of always out of my seat, or calling out, or rushing ahead before they’d even finished the directions. I don’t mean it like I was trying to be a problem, but yeah… it was happening way before middle school, not just since I started radiology training.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble paying attention to details or making careless mistakes?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think that’s the main thing I notice, if that makes sense. I try to be careful, especially with anything important, and I don’t want to say I’m making a bunch of mistakes if I’m not.\n\nCould you maybe say what kind of details you mean? Like schoolwork, or at work?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others or blurt out answers before they've finished speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, sometimes. I try not to, because I know it probably comes off rude. Like if a supervisor is explaining a positioning step or asking me something, I’ll jump in with what I think the answer is before they’re done talking. And then I’ll realize I missed the part that actually mattered.  \n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly—I’m not trying to cut people off. It just kind of comes out before I stop myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or managing your time?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try to keep things together. I wouldn’t want to say I’m just disorganized across the board. Sometimes I probably cut it close with timing, but I usually find a way to get things done.\n\nWhen you say “organizing tasks,” do you mean like school assignments and scheduling, or more like steps during work? I’m not sure how much of it counts as a problem versus just me needing a better system.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking, like grabbing something or doing something physical without considering the consequences?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think I’m, like, grabbing things in a dangerous way or anything. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often fidget with your hands or feet or feel restless when you're sitting or standing still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one is probably more of an issue for me. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but when I’m supposed to sit still in the radiology suite or in class, I feel really restless, like I’m crawling out of my skin.\n\nI’ll shift around a lot, move my feet, or find some excuse to get up or pace if I can. I’ve tried to just tough it out for months, but it still happens. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when it's not your turn or when others are trying to speak?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not really. I don’t think the attention part is as much of a problem, honestly. I mean, I’m sure I’m not perfect, but the thing that really made me come in is more the restlessness.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah—thanks. I guess I’m here because I’ve been having a really hard time sitting still, like in the radiology suite or in class. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it kind of feels like I’m crawling out of my skin when I’m supposed to stay put. I’ve tried to just push through it for months, but I still end up pacing, shifting around, or finding some reason to get up and move. I’m not sure if that makes sense, but it’s been getting hard to manage.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice you were having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I think it goes back pretty far, honestly. My parents still bring up my elementary school conferences—teachers would say I was kind of always out of my seat, or calling out, or rushing ahead before they’d even finished the directions. I don’t mean it like I was trying to be a problem, but yeah… it was happening way before middle school, not just since I started radiology training.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble paying attention to details or making careless mistakes?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think that’s the main thing I notice, if that makes sense. I try to be careful, especially with anything important, and I don’t want to say I’m making a bunch of mistakes if I’m not.\n\nCould you maybe say what kind of details you mean? Like schoolwork, or at work?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others or blurt out answers before they've finished speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, sometimes. I try not to, because I know it probably comes off rude. Like if a supervisor is explaining a positioning step or asking me something, I’ll jump in with what I think the answer is before they’re done talking. And then I’ll realize I missed the part that actually mattered.  \n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly—I’m not trying to cut people off. It just kind of comes out before I stop myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or managing your time?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try to keep things together. I wouldn’t want to say I’m just disorganized across the board. Sometimes I probably cut it close with timing, but I usually find a way to get things done.\n\nWhen you say “organizing tasks,” do you mean like school assignments and scheduling, or more like steps during work? I’m not sure how much of it counts as a problem versus just me needing a better system.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking, like grabbing something or doing something physical without considering the consequences?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think I’m, like, grabbing things in a dangerous way or anything. At least not that I’ve really noticed.\n\nI can be quick to do something before thinking it all the way through, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean. Like, do you mean physically doing stuff without checking first, or more like just reacting too fast?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself getting distracted or quitting tasks before you finish them?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I can get distracted sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s more than normal or if I’m just tired or busy.\n\nQuitting tasks before I finish them… I try not to. If something has to get done, I usually push myself to finish it. I might switch around a little or need to come back to things, but I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often fidget with your hands or feet or feel restless when you're sitting or standing still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one is probably more of an issue for me. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but when I’m supposed to sit still in the radiology suite or in class, I feel really restless, like I’m crawling out of my skin.\n\nI’ll shift around a lot, move my feet, or find some excuse to get up or pace if I can. I’ve tried to just tough it out for months, but it still happens. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when it's not your turn or when others are trying to speak?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I can talk a lot if I’m nervous or if I’m really into what we’re talking about, but I try pretty hard not to interrupt people.\n\nI’m not sure if other people would say I do that more than I realize, though. It’s not something I’ve really thought of as a major issue, I guess. My parents still bring up my elementary school conferences—teachers would say I was kind of always out of my seat, or calling out, or rushing ahead before they’d even finished the directions. I don’t mean it like I was trying to be a problem, but yeah… it was happening way before middle school, not just since I started radiology training.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble paying attention to details or making careless mistakes?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think that’s the main thing I notice, if that makes sense. Like schoolwork, or at work?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others or blurt out answers before they've finished speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, sometimes. I try not to, because I know it probably comes off rude. And then I’ll realize I missed the part that actually mattered.  \n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly—I’m not trying to cut people off. It just kind of comes out before I stop myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or managing your time?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try to keep things together. I wouldn’t want to say I’m just disorganized across the board. I’m not sure how much of it counts as a problem versus just me needing a better system.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking, like grabbing something or doing something physical without considering the consequences?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think I’m, like, grabbing things in a dangerous way or anything. At least not that I’ve really noticed.\n\nI can be quick to do something before thinking it all the way through, but I’m not sure if that’s what you mean. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often fidget with your hands or feet or feel restless when you're sitting or standing still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one is probably more of an issue for me. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but when I’m supposed to sit still in the radiology suite or in class, I feel really restless, like I’m crawling out of my skin.\n\nI’ll shift around a lot, move my feet, or find some excuse to get up or pace if I can. I’ve tried to just tough it out for months, but it still happens. Like, do you mean physically doing stuff without checking first, or more like just reacting too fast?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself getting distracted or quitting tasks before you finish them?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I can get distracted sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s more than normal or if I’m just tired or busy.\n\nQuitting tasks before I finish them… I try not to. If something has to get done, I usually push myself to finish it. I might switch around a little or need to come back to things, but I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often fidget with your hands or feet or feel restless when you're sitting or standing still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that one is probably more of an issue for me. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but when I’m supposed to sit still in the radiology suite or in class, I feel really restless, like I’m crawling out of my skin.\n\nI’ll shift around a lot, move my feet, or find some excuse to get up or pace if I can. I’ve tried to just tough it out for months, but it still happens. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when it's not your turn or when others are trying to speak?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I can talk a lot if I’m nervous or if I’m really into what we’re talking about, but I try pretty hard not to interrupt people.\n\nI’m not sure if other people would say I do that more than I realize, though. I don’t want to answer it wrong. Like, I haven’t noticed it being a big problem, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have strong emotions that are hard to control?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I get frustrated or stressed like anyone, but I don’t know that I’d say my emotions are “out of control.”\n\nSometimes I probably feel things pretty strongly, but I try to keep it to myself and not make it other people’s problem. I guess I’d want to know what you mean by hard to control—like yelling, crying, shutting down, that kind of thing?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on something that you get completely absorbed in it and lose track of everything else?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I can get into something if it’s interesting, like anyone, and then realize some time passed… but I don’t know if it’s to the point of losing track of everything else.\n\nI wouldn’t want to overstate it. It’s not something I’ve really thought of as a major issue, I guess. What kind of situations do you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget things you're supposed to do, like appointments or chores?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try not to. I use reminders on my phone for appointments and stuff, so I usually manage okay.\n\nChores are kind of harder to judge because sometimes I’ll just put them off or forget until someone reminds me, but I don’t know if that’s more than normal. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble keeping your workspace or living space organized?\n\nPatient: I mean… it’s not perfect, but I don’t know if I’d call it a big problem. My room or desk can get kind of messy when I’m busy, but I usually know where the important stuff is.\n\nI try to clean it up when it starts looking bad, especially if someone might see it. Is that the kind of thing you mean, like messy, or more like not being able to find things?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your main problem is paying attention or sitting still and controlling your movements?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I think it’s more the sitting still part. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but that’s kind of why I came in.\n\nLike in the radiology suite or in class, when I’m supposed to just sit or stay in one spot, I start feeling really restless, almost like I’m crawling out of my skin. I try to tough it out because I know I’m supposed to be professional, but I still end up shifting around, pacing if I can, or finding some excuse to get up and move. It’s been going on long enough that I guess I can’t just say it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with sitting still is more of a problem for you than your ability to pay attention to details?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think sitting still is the bigger problem for me. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m careless with details or anything, because I do try, but the reason I came in is more that feeling like I’m crawling out of my skin when I’m supposed to stay seated or stay in one spot.\n\nLike in the radiology suite or in class, I’ll tell myself to just deal with it, but after a while I’m shifting around, or I need to pace if I can, or I find some excuse to move. I’ve tried to tough it out for months, so… yeah, I think that’s more of the issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with paying attention is as much of a problem for you as your difficulty with sitting still?\n\nPatient: I don’t think the attention part is as much of a problem, honestly. I mean, I’m sure I’m not perfect, but the thing that really made me come in is more the restlessness.\n\nWhen I’m in the radiology suite or sitting in class and I’m supposed to stay still, I start feeling like I’m crawling out of my skin. I’ve tried for months to just tough it out and not make it a big deal, but I still end up shifting around, pacing if I can, or kind of finding some reason to get up and move. So… yeah, I’d say sitting still is the bigger issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with sitting still and restlessness for as long as you can remember?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I didn’t really think of it as a “problem” back then, if that makes sense, but my parents still bring up elementary school conferences where teachers said I was always out of my seat or calling out.\n\nThey’d also say I’d rush ahead before the directions were even finished. So I guess it wasn’t just something that started with radiology training or college. It was happening way before middle school.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with sitting still and restlessness caused problems for you in your relationships or at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but it has started causing problems, especially at work or in the program.\n\nMy clinical evaluations have gone down, and I was put on a formal remediation plan. My supervisors have said that when I’m restless or getting up and moving around, it can disrupt patient flow and make it harder for the team to work smoothly. That’s… honestly kind of embarrassing to say.\n\nI’m trying to take it seriously, because I’m worried if I don’t get it under control, it could affect whether I’m able to stay in the program.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but when I’m supposed to sit still in the radiology suite or in class, I feel really restless, like I’m crawling out of my skin.\n\nI’ll shift around a lot, move my feet, or find some excuse to get up or pace if I can. I’ve tried to just tough it out for months, but it still happens. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when it's not your turn or when others are trying to speak?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I can talk a lot if I’m nervous or if I’m really into what we’re talking about, but I try pretty hard not to interrupt people.\n\nI’m not sure if other people would say I do that more than I realize, though. I don’t want to answer it wrong. Like, I haven’t noticed it being a big problem, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have strong emotions that are hard to control?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I get frustrated or stressed like anyone, but I don’t know that I’d say my emotions are “out of control.”\n\nSometimes I probably feel things pretty strongly, but I try to keep it to myself and not make it other people’s problem. I guess I’d want to know what you mean by hard to control—like yelling, crying, shutting down, that kind of thing?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on something that you get completely absorbed in it and lose track of everything else?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I can get into something if it’s interesting, like anyone, and then realize some time passed… but I don’t know if it’s to the point of losing track of everything else.\n\nI wouldn’t want to overstate it. It’s not something I’ve really thought of as a major issue, I guess. What kind of situations do you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget things you're supposed to do, like appointments or chores?\n\nPatient: I mean… I try not to. I use reminders on my phone for appointments and stuff, so I usually manage okay.\n\nChores are kind of harder to judge because sometimes I’ll just put them off or forget until someone reminds me, but I don’t know if that’s more than normal. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble keeping your workspace or living space organized?\n\nPatient: I mean… it’s not perfect, but I don’t know if I’d call it a big problem. My room or desk can get kind of messy when I’m busy, but I usually know where the important stuff is.\n\nI try to clean it up when it starts looking bad, especially if someone might see it. Is that the kind of thing you mean, like messy, or more like not being able to find things?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your main problem is paying attention or sitting still and controlling your movements?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I think it’s more the sitting still part. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but that’s kind of why I came in.\n\nLike in the radiology suite or in class, when I’m supposed to just sit or stay in one spot, I start feeling really restless, almost like I’m crawling out of my skin. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m careless with details or anything, because I do try, but the reason I came in is more that feeling like I’m crawling out of my skin when I’m supposed to stay seated or stay in one spot.\n\nLike in the radiology suite or in class, I’ll tell myself to just deal with it, but after a while I’m shifting around, or I need to pace if I can, or I find some excuse to move. I’ve tried to tough it out for months, so… yeah, I think that’s more of the issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with paying attention is as much of a problem for you as your difficulty with sitting still?\n\nPatient: I don’t think the attention part is as much of a problem, honestly. I mean, I’m sure I’m not perfect, but the thing that really made me come in is more the restlessness.\n\nWhen I’m in the radiology suite or sitting in class and I’m supposed to stay still, I start feeling like I’m crawling out of my skin. I’ve tried for months to just tough it out and not make it a big deal, but I still end up shifting around, pacing if I can, or kind of finding some reason to get up and move. So… yeah, I’d say sitting still is the bigger issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with sitting still and restlessness for as long as you can remember?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. So I guess it wasn’t just something that started with radiology training or college. It was happening way before middle school.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with sitting still and restlessness caused problems for you in your relationships or at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but it has started causing problems, especially at work or in the program.\n\nMy clinical evaluations have gone down, and I was put on a formal remediation plan. My supervisors have said that when I’m restless or getting up and moving around, it can disrupt patient flow and make it harder for the team to work smoothly. That’s… honestly kind of embarrassing to say.\n\nI’m trying to take it seriously, because I’m worried if I don’t get it under control, it could affect whether I’m able to stay in the program.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your restlessness and difficulty sitting still are the main symptoms that are causing you problems?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so.
